(AP-167) *Superseded by D-808: a retype only widens, nothing is converted.* |
| D-228 | Deleting a property, and markers of existing ACs | 3D offers only `Keep values in notes`; `Remove values from <n> notes` disabled `Coming in MS4`. Mark links.md FR-TYP-04 AC-1…AC-3 `[MS4]`; change FR-STO-04 / FR-TYP-07 AC-3 to `[MS3]` (verified by FR-TYP-07 AC-5). Both changes need `AC-Test-Change` approval if tests exist. (AP-168) |
| D-229 | What the attribute panel shows, in which order | Schema attributes in schema order (empty ones as `Empty`), then Tags, Aliases, Source, Review (when on or present), Created, Modified (read-only); description as the editable subtitle; Tags row = frontmatter `tags` only (inline tags stay in the body); Other properties collapsed, read-only in 3D. (AP-169) |
| D-230 | How edited values are written | Clearing removes the key; the key's text and quoting are kept; flow lists stay flow and quoted scalars keep their quote style when YAML allows; text YAML would read as another type is double-quoted; numbers accept the region's decimal comma and group spaces, written with `.`; a date-time gets the offset of that moment in the machine's zone; a non-existent local time is refused; an ambiguous one takes the first occurrence. (AP-170) |
| D-231 | Invalid values and the required flag | Invalid values are shown raw with a warning and kept byte for byte until the user commits a valid value. Required only marks the label and warns when empty; it never blocks saving, navigation or type changes. (AP-171) |
| D-232 | Defaults, templates and review when creating | One core creation path for all entry points. Order `id, type, title`, schema attributes (template value, else default, else omitted), other template keys, `review: unreviewed` when on or locked, `created, modified`, then the template body. The type file names its default template (`template: _templates/<Name>/<Name>.md`). Starter templates are body-only; defaults live in the type file. (AP-172) |
| D-233 | The type page in 3D (and AP-19) | A read-only table: Title, the type's preview attributes, Modified; default sort Modified descending, sortable headers, sort remembered per machine; a title filter; `Add column` adds a property (and a preview column); `Filter`, `Group`, `Add view` disabled v1.0; no Links column until computed columns are decided with FR-QRY (keep AP-19 open). Supersedes app-shell FR-UX-02 AC-17's title sort. (AP-173) |
| D-234 | ⌘N on a type page | ⌘N stays the default type (D-131); the type page's `New <Name>` button shows no shortcut and is in the palette as `New <Name>`. (AP-174) |
| D-235 | Writing type files, and their version (AR-07) | Patch only the changed lines (like frontmatter); write with a base-version check and reload with a notice on conflict; missing `version` = 1; a newer version is read-only (notes stay editable); invalid files are never rewritten. The new keys are optional additions to version 1 (nothing has been released), so the version stays 1. (AP-175) |
| D-236 | Colours and icons | The eight token colours (R14), labelled as in R14; the 24 icon names of R14; unknown names fall back without rewriting. Update the board. (AP-176) |
| D-237 | What can change on system types | Editable: icon, colour, folder (new objects only), description, preview, review (unless locked), own attributes. Locked: name, plural, built-in attributes (incl. Task status labels until MS5). (AP-177) (Tag: no own attributes in the MVP, D-693) |
| D-238 | Starter types' attributes (D-92 leaves them to MS3's ACs) | Approve R11 and the overlay fixture `tests/fixtures/vaults/new-starter/` (English names and keys, review off, body-only templates), like D-99. (AP-178) |
| D-239 | Matching `type` values | Match by key (NFC, lower case, whitespace as `-`); a value that isn't a single string, or is empty, is shown as Page with a warning; never normalized on disk until the user changes the type. (AP-179) |
| D-240 | Undo for attribute and type edits | A committed panel edit or type change is one step of the note's undo history (⌘Z with the focus in the body or the panel outside an open field); in an open text field ⌘Z is the field's own undo. (AP-180) |

| D-245 | New-object bytes (D-82 vs D-232) | D-82 holds: a new object's bytes come from `@tegg/doc` `newObject`, called by one UI function `createObject` that every entry point uses (D-232's one path); the core command `note_create(type, title, text)` picks the folder and free name, writes and indexes. FR-TYP-03 AC-12 checks the bytes at L1, the write at L4, each entry point at L5 (owner, 2026-10-14; ms3-shell §14 Q-3D-1) |

| D-247 | Focus on open (F4) | Opening an existing note puts the cursor in the first body block; a new note focuses the title (owner, 2026-10-14) |
| D-248 | `source` as free text (F8) | The reserved key `source` (FR-TYP-15) is shown and edited as a free-text property on every type; importers keep writing it (owner, 2026-10-14) |

| D-251 | Defining an undefined type (F12) | The page of a `type` value without a definition (D-121) offers `Create type "<value>"`: it writes `.tegg/types/<key>.yml` (name from the value, plural and folder editable, D-222 rules, no attributes), after which its objects show the panel; no note file is written. | D-253 | Multi-word search tiers (AP-185) | Unquoted words: tier 1 exact phrase, tier 2 all words, tier 3 any word; inside a tier exact words before typo matches, then title/file name, attribute, body (D-107), then relevance. Quoted text stays a strict phrase; `-word` still excludes (owner, 2026-10-15). Order amended by D-1341 (v1.140): title-first groups come before these tiers |
| D-254 | File names in search (AP-186) | The file stem only (no folder, no `.md`), NFC and folded, ranked as a title match; a hit matched only by its file name shows `File name: <stem>` with the highlight (owner, 2026-10-15) |
| D-255 | Caret on open, details (AP-187) | Caret at the start of the first editable body block, without scrolling; empty body → the empty paragraph; read-only files or nothing editable → D-130 heading focus; the body textbox is described by the title; a search result or heading link puts the caret at the match/heading. | D-274 | Using backup zips (D-57) | Settings → Backups lists the newest 10 with date, operation and size, and `Reveal in Finder`. Restoring is manual in the MVP (unzip over a copy of the vault); one-click restore comes with version history (v1.0) (AP-202) |

| D-276 | Backlinks order (FR-LNK-01) | Groups follow the sidebar's type order (D-121), undefined types last by raw value; a type with no linking objects has no group. Inside a group, most recently modified first (by instant), entries without `modified` last by title (D-122) (AP-204) |
| D-277 | Backlink excerpts (FR-LNK-01) | The excerpt is the block containing the link: a paragraph, a heading's text, a list item's own text (not its children), a table row with cells joined by ` · `, a callout's or quote's paragraph. At most 160 characters with at most 60 before the link, cut at whitespace, `…` at cut ends; inline formatting rendered, markers and comments hidden, chips styled and the ones pointing here marked; no heading breadcrumb (AP-205) |
| D-278 | What counts as a backlink (FR-LNK-01) | One entry per linking object, one excerpt per block that links here, in file order with frontmatter first; 3 shown, then `Show <n> more` (not remembered). Not backlinks: self-links, sources in `_templates/` or `.trash/`, inline `#tags` and `tags:`, unparsable frontmatter (that file's body links count). A link to a missing heading or block id counts and is not broken until FR-LNK-06 (AP-206) |

Create opens the new note with the caret in its empty body; Back returns to the link; ⌘Z never deletes the file (D-178) (AP-214) |
| D-287 | Targets that aren't a file name as written | Create writes the sanitized file name with the full title and changes only the clicked link in the open note to `[[<stem>\|<target>]]` through the editor (one undo step); the offer says so first. | D-291 | One resolution rule; vault-wide free stems | (1) Backlinks, broken links and every index query resolve each link with the `resolve_links` rules from its source note (same folder first, relative Markdown links, dates first), re-resolved when files appear, disappear or move; a property test checks backlinks are the inverse of `link_resolve`. (2) Every creation path, and 4E's type moves, picks a file stem unused anywhere in the vault outside `_templates/` and `.trash/`, NFC and case-insensitive; existing same-stem files are left as they are (AP-219) |

(3) WebKit (L5) tests run fully parallel with 8 workers under `./verify`; the route sweeps of FR-UX-03 AC-1, AC-2, AC-8, AC-9 are split into one test per route or shard with the same assertions. Precondition: the nightly job is installed, else (1) lowers coverage |

Tokens are removed from the title; anything else stays in the title verbatim. The row shows the parsed due date and priority before ↩. The section the row sits in presets the due date (Today & overdue → today, This week → the last shown day, Not scheduled → none) |
| D-381 | Recurrence badge stays v1.0 (AP-k, owner, 2026-10-16) | The `monthly` / `weekly` badges on task cards and the Done hint about recurring instances belong to FR-TSK-07 (v1.0) and stay on the boards dimmed as a v1.0 marker; slice 5A shows no recurrence and writes no `recurrence` key |
| D-382 | Real-app tests in every slice (TS-I7; owner, 2026-10-16) | Slices 4A and 4B and the D-369 feedback shipped without the L6 part of five ACs (FR-LNK-04 AC-5, AC-12, FR-DLY-02 AC-2, FR-DLY-04 AC-6, FR-TYP-07 AC-11), because the coverage report counted an AC as tested when any layer referenced it. From now on the red AC tests of a slice include an `app/e2e/` test for every AC whose layer marker names L6, and `./verify push` fails the `l6-coverage` check when an L6 AC is tested elsewhere but not in `app/e2e/` (`tools/qa/CONTRACT.md` §9.5). L6 ACs with no test yet (deferred, e.g. `[MS8]` benchmarks) don't fail it. The five missing tests were added with owner approval (`AC-Test-Change`). |
| D-383 | Form of the rewritten link (AP-288, owner, 2026-10-17) | Keep each link's form: path stays path-qualified, `.md` stays, Markdown links get the new percent-encoded relative URL and keep their label text; an alias exactly equal to the old title is replaced by the new title (or dropped when the new title equals the new stem); every other alias is kept. A heading/block link to a title that needs an alias gets the alias too (`[[stem#Úvod\|title]]`); embeds never get one. |
| D-384 | Target stem taken (AP-289, owner, 2026-10-17) | A rename uses the same vault-wide free-stem rule: `Gama (2)`, `(3)`…; a case-only change of the note's own stem is not a collision. |
| D-385 | Undo after later changes (AP-290, owner, 2026-10-17) | Undo is a job with base versions: files unchanged since the rename get their pre-rename bytes; changed ones are skipped and listed (`Link not updated`, Retry reverses only the link). Undo is available until the app quits or the vault switches; one undo entry per rename in the renamed note's history plus `Undo rename` in the palette for the last one. |
| D-386 | Linking note open with unsaved edits (AP-291, owner, 2026-10-17) | The job sends the transformation for the open note to its editor (applied as a transaction to the live document, saved by the next autosave, one undo step in that note), instead of writing the file directly. |
| D-387 | Crash mid-rename without a zip (D-269) (AP-292, owner, 2026-10-17) | Persist a small job journal in Application Support (op, file list, pre-job bytes of affected files; not a zip, not in the 10-zip rotation, deleted when the job ends). On the next launch show `Renaming "<old>" was interrupted: <k> of <n> links updated` with `Finish` (rewrite the rest) and `Undo`. |
| D-388 | Links in non-relation frontmatter values (AP-293, owner, 2026-10-17) | Rewrite `[[…]]` in every frontmatter string value the index extracts links from (schema keys and unknown keys alike), touching only the link bytes. |
| D-389 | Self-links and the renamed note's own write (AP-294, owner, 2026-10-17) | The note's `title:` change, its self-links and the file rename are one atomic step (write new bytes to the new name with `RENAME_EXCL`, then remove the old name); the index never sees both files as notes. |
| D-390 | Notes whose stem never matched their title (AP-295, owner, 2026-10-17) | Yes, always rename to `sanitize(new title)` with the free-stem rule; the `(2)` suffix disappears when the name is free. |
| D-391 | Templates and trash (AP-296, owner, 2026-10-17) | Rewrite links in `_templates/` (they produce future notes); never touch `.trash/`; a title edit in a template or trashed note writes only `title:` (no rename). |
| D-392 | Does merge (D-290) ship in 4C (AP-297, owner, 2026-10-17) | Merge's contract is in `ms4-jobs.md` now; its UI and ACs ship with 4D-lite (the first zip-backed client), and the `Merge…` button is relabelled `Coming in MS4 (4D)`. |
| D-393 | Cancel and concurrency for a rename without confirmation (AP-298, owner, 2026-10-17) | Note rename: cancel only during `planning`; Undo is the remedy. Jobs run one at a time in a FIFO queue; a second rename of a note that is in a running job waits for it. |
| D-394 | Autosave of the title while typing (AP-299, owner, 2026-10-17) | Keep autosaving `title:` while typing (no data loss on crash); the commit then renames and rewrites links. Links/aliases are compared against the title at the start of the edit, not the autosaved intermediate. |
| D-395 | Undo for zip-backed jobs (AP-300, owner, 2026-10-17) | Only note rename has undo in the MVP; all zip-backed jobs restore from the zip (Settings › Backups); merge has no undo (D-290). |
| D-396 | When the restore UI ships (AP-301, owner, 2026-10-17) | Build the zip engine in 4C (tested at L4 with a test-only job) and the Settings › Backups restore UI with the first zip-backed client (4D-lite tag rename). |
| D-397 | No performance target for jobs (AP-302, owner, 2026-10-17) | Report-only benchmark in 4C (500 backlinks on the generated vault, D-152); the owner sets a target at the MS4 gate. |
| D-398 | Where the "link not updated" list lives and how long (AP-303, owner, 2026-10-17) | In-memory, per vault, until quit: a status-area entry `<n> links not updated` opening a non-modal list with Open/Retry/Retry all, plus the palette `Show links not updated`. Not written to the vault or Application Support. |
| D-399 | No external-rename link offer in 4C (owner, 2026-10-17) | D-294's "Update links to the renamed note" after an external rename is not built in 4C; the planner supports retargeting from a stale stem, and the offer comes with Broken links in v1.0 |
| D-400 | Requirement headings in rename.md (AP-304, owner, 2026-10-17) | rename.md's requirement headings become `###`, so `verify coverage`, `l6-coverage` and `milestone-acs` track its criteria. |
| D-401 | AC numbering clash with links.md (AP-305, owner, 2026-10-17) | rename.md's FR-FMT-08 AC-5…AC-10 keep their numbers; links.md's FR-LNK-03 AC-1…AC-6 keep theirs. Tests name rename.md's criteria `fr_fmt_08_ac5…` and links.md's `fr_lnk_03_ac1…ac6`, so no two tests share an ID. |
| D-402 | Case-only rename and free stems (AP-306, owner, 2026-10-17) | A rename whose new stem has the same key (NFC, case-folded) as the note's own current stem is never a collision: `alfa` → `Alfa` keeps the stem, only its case changes, even when other files share the stem elsewhere in the vault. |
| D-403 | Unquoted wikilinks in frontmatter (AP-307, owner, 2026-10-17) | An unquoted `key: [[Alfa]]` (YAML reads it as a nested list) is rewritten like a string value: only the target bytes change, the YAML form stays (FR-LNK-03 AC-8). |
| D-404 | Sync-simulator job points (AP-308, owner, 2026-10-17) | The engine exposes named points `AfterPlanning`, `AfterRead`, `TempWritten` with a test hook (`Jobs::set_point_hook`, test builds and Rust tests only). For the `TempWritten` point the job writes the temp file and renames it itself instead of using the single `VaultFs::replace` call. |
| D-405 | Contract gaps filled by the stubs (AP-309, owner, 2026-10-17) | The stubs' choices become the contract: no `to: N` in `RetargetLinks`; Rust `SetTitle` carries `now`; `JobError::Core(String)` (no crate cycle); the IPC's `fromTitle` reaches the planner through `Jobs::start_note_rename(path, title, from_title)`, while `JobOp::NoteRename` uses the stored title; `JobTransform` carries `live`; `editor_open`/`editor_close`/`job_transform_result` names; `NodeTransformer::start(script)`/`from_repo(root)` with JSON lines `{id, source, text, ops}` → `{id, result | error}`. |
| D-406 | Skip-reason texts (AP-310, owner, 2026-10-17) | Catalog texts: `job.skip.missing` = `missing`, `job.skip.readOnly` = `read-only`, `job.skip.io` = `could not be written`, `job.rename.cancel` = `Cancel`. |
| D-407 | Self-link and "body unchanged" (AP-311, owner, 2026-10-17) | FR-FMT-08 AC-5's "body bytes unchanged" excludes the note's self-links, which the same write rewrites (FR-LNK-03 AC-7). |
| D-408 | Links "resolved through an alias" (AP-312, owner, 2026-10-17) | Links never resolve by alias (D-291). FR-LNK-03 AC-10's alias case means: a `[[<alias>]]` link in another note is not rewritten, and its resolution is the same before and after the rename. |
| D-409 | `queued` phase in progress events (AP-313, owner, 2026-10-17) | FR-UX-07 AC-8's phase list is extended with `queued`; the 500 ms rule is measured at the receiver with 100 ms slack. |
| D-410 | "Index was stale" in FR-SYN-06 AC-10 (AP-314, owner, 2026-10-17) | A file whose bytes changed since planning is skipped as `changed` (§5); FR-SYN-06 AC-10 covers only files whose rewritten bytes equal their current bytes (counted `unchanged`). Its "stale index" example is removed. |
| D-411 | Temp-file guard and banner count in FR-LNK-03 AC-17 (AP-315, owner, 2026-10-17) | The crash test ages leftover temp files past FR-SYN-02 AC-4's one-minute guard before reopening (as D-344). `<done> of <total>` in the interrupted banner counts files, and its text says `notes`. |
| D-412 | Skipped file at L6 (AP-316, owner, 2026-10-17) | FR-LNK-03 AC-16's L6 case produces its skipped file by making a folder read-only (reason `io`); the external-edit case stays at L4/L5. |
| D-413 | Markdown-link URLs gaining spaces; `sanitize` at L1 (AP-317, owner, 2026-10-17) | A Markdown-link URL that was neither percent-encoded nor in `<…>` and gains a space is written percent-encoded (`%20`). `sanitize` is tested through the Rust `sanitize_file_stem` (no TS twin). |
| D-414 | FR-FMT-08 AC-5 without the inode (owner, 2026-10-17) | While the title is typed, `Pages/Alfa.md` keeps its path, not its inode: the autosave of `title:` (D-394) is an atomic temp-file write and rename (FR-SYN-02), which always gives a new inode. | D-415 | FR-EMB-03 for note embeds in MS4 (AP-318, owner, 2026-10-17) | FR-EMB-03 moves to MS4 for note embeds: full mode is editable in place in 4F (D-273). FR-CNV-05 (editing a converted task's status in place) stays MS5 |
| D-416 | Per-embed mode in the alias slot (AP-319, owner, 2026-10-17) | An embed may override its type's default: `![[Note\|full]]`, `\|card`, `\|link` (read case-insensitively, written lower case). Other text after `\|` is kept and ignored for the mode; choosing the type's default removes the keyword; a change replaces other text after `\|` (undoable). Markdown dialect §5 |
| D-417 | Per-type embed default (AP-320, owner, 2026-10-17) | Type-file key `embed: full \| card \| link`, after `hidden`/`preview`. Absent or unknown → built-in default: Page, Quick Note, Daily Note full; Task, Tag, Image, PDF, user types and undefined types card. An unknown value is shown with a warning and never rewritten; the key is written only when the user changes the setting (D-90); starter types unchanged |
| D-418 | Link-mode embed look (AP-321, owner, 2026-10-17) | A link-mode embed is an inline chip with the embed look: 1 px solid `embed-border` border and `embed-bg` background (link chips have no border and the link tint, D-73), the type icon, accessible description `Embedded note` (FR-EMB-02, FR-LNK-07) |
| D-419 | Where full and card embeds appear (AP-322, owner, 2026-10-17) | Only a paragraph holding nothing but the embed, at the top level of a body (the host's or an embedded note's), is shown full or card. Every other embed (in text, list items, quotes, callouts, headings, table cells) is link mode, whatever its default or override |
| D-420 | Embed depth and circularity (AP-323, owner, 2026-10-17) | Only full mode expands, so only it adds depth and can be circular. Depth 1 = an embed in the open note; content shows for depths 1–3; an embed inside a depth-3 full embed is link mode marked `Embedded too deeply to show here`. A full embed whose target file (by path, whatever its subpath) is the open note or an ancestor frame is a circular frame (header, Open, message), never expanded; repeats in sibling branches aren't circular (FR-EMB-04) |
| D-421 | Editing embeds in place (AP-324, owner, 2026-10-17) | Editable: depth-1 full embeds of a whole note or a `#Heading` section, in the editor. Read-only: block embeds, depths 2–3, cards, link mode, everything in the read view, files NFR-REL-03 opens read-only (`Read-only`). Each editable embed is a nested editor on its file with its own base version, autosave (D-179), conflict and deleted dialogs naming the note, and undo history (separate from the host's). Leaving an embed flushes its save; other instances of the file reload. The Save state covers every file of the page (FR-EMB-03, D-273) |
| D-422 | Heading section embeds (AP-325, owner, 2026-10-17) | `![[Note#Heading]]` shows the blocks after the first heading matching (NFC, trimmed, case-insensitive, as D-140) up to the next heading of the same or higher level or the end of the file. The heading shows only in the header (`Note › Heading`, D-156) and isn't editable there. A same-or-higher heading typed inside ends the section at the next save; the text stays in the file |
| D-423 | Embed clicks before previews (AP-326, owner, 2026-10-17) | As D-140: a click or ⏎ on a card or link-mode embed opens the target; the header's `Open <title>` opens it (with the `#Heading` fragment); a click in a full embed's body places the caret; ⌘↩ on a selected embed opens it. No preview control until FR-LNK-09 (v1.0) (superseded by slice P, D-921…D-944) |
| D-424 | Embeds that can't show content (AP-327, owner, 2026-10-17) | (1) Unresolved: a broken frame (dashed `danger` border, no tint, no icon) whose header is D-310's unresolved chip with the Create offer; after Create it renders in the new note's mode. (2) Missing heading or block id: a normal frame with a message, not broken (D-278). (3) A date with no Daily Note: a Daily Note frame `No note for this day yet.`; Open goes to the virtual note, nothing written. (4) Invalid frontmatter or not UTF-8: body read-only with `Read-only`. (5) Empty body: `Empty note`. Supersedes D-310's "stays an R5 placeholder" |
| D-425 | Task card in 4F (AP-328, owner, 2026-10-17) | The Task embed card in 4F is the generic card: header (Task icon, title, Open) and the preview strip (`status`, `priority`, `due`), read-only; no checkbox and no body until MS5 builds D-74's card (D-273) |
| D-426 | Embed insertion (AP-329, owner, 2026-10-17) | Slash label `Embed object`. The picker uses the switcher's matching and `Create "<query>"` (D-106) and writes `![[<target>]]` with D-184's target (stem, else vault path), never an alias. Typing `![[` opens the same suggestions and writes the same form. Heading embeds are typed by hand in 4F. `Block embed` moves to v1.0 with FR-LNK-06; existing `#^id` embeds render read-only |
| D-427 | Card content (AP-330, owner, 2026-10-17) | A card is the header plus the type's preview properties that are set, in preview order, each `<Name> <value>` formatted as on the type page (D-160 dates, relations as chips); no body or description (D-313); a card with none set shows the header only (FR-EMB-02, FR-TYP-11) |
| D-428 | Embed keyboard model (AP-331, owner, 2026-10-17) | In the editor an embed is a block (selected, moved, duplicated, deleted whole, bytes verbatim). With it selected: ⏎ enters a full embed, ⌘↩ opens the target, `Display embed as…` opens the mode menu; inside, Esc returns with the embed selected, ↑ on the first line and ↓ on the last leave it. In the read view `Open` and `Display as` are Tab stops. Palette commands without shortcuts: `Embed object…`, `Open embedded note`, `Display embed as…` (FR-UX-03) |
| D-429 | Embed loading (AP-332, owner, 2026-10-17) | One embed-content call per nesting level for all embeds of that level, each file read at most once per open, from the files (AR-01); showing an embed records no recency (D-138); `note-ready` waits for the host and the embed headers, not their content; all depth-1 content painted within 112 ms of `note-ready`, report-only in MS4 (D-152), enforced from MS8 |
| D-430 | 4F contract (AP-333, owner, 2026-10-17) | `docs/contracts/ms4-embeds.md` is written before the red tests (as D-295), with a batched `embed_load(from, targets[])` per level, `TypeOp::SetEmbed`, section and mode helpers in `@tegg/doc`, the editor's embed node, and fake-core support |
| D-431 | Criteria changed by 4F (AP-334, owner, 2026-10-17) | read-view R5 (`embed-note`) and FR-EDT-02 AC-13, editor FR-EDT-02 AC-21 and R6, backlinks FR-LNK-04 AC-6 wording and R16, types R12 and app-shell R4 are updated in the 4F build; test changes carry `AC-Test-Change: 4F replaces the embed-note placeholder (D-144, D-310); approved-by: owner`. embeds.md's FR-FMT-07 and FR-SYN-06 criteria are renumbered if 4C merges first |
| D-432 | Embed drag moves to v1.0 (AP-335, owner, 2026-10-17) | FR-EMB-01's drag from the sidebar or search moves to v1.0 (as the daily-use plan). Until then a drop on a note does nothing and writes nothing |
| D-433 | Links never move to a name the note isn't at (owner, 2026-10-17; PR #45 review M1–M3) | (1) Undo and Redo move the renamed note first; if it can't move (changed since, or another note took the target name) no linking file is changed and all are listed as not updated. An interrupted Undo follows the same rule. (2) Retry accepts only rows the job skipped, never after the rename was undone or when its note is no longer at the new name; an undo removes that rename's rows from the list. (3) After a rename takes a free stem (`Gama (2)`), the crash journal's target follows it, so Finish moves pending links there, never to the planned name. (Numbered after 4F's D-415…D-432.) |
| D-434 | Duplicated embed lines keep their bytes (AP-336, owner, 2026-10-17) | Duplicating, moving or changing the mode of an embed block keeps the bytes the file had (NFD included): raw blocks are never re-normalized. FR-FMT-12's NFC rule applies to targets the app writes (FR-EMB-01 AC-13, FR-FMT-07 AC-15) |
| D-435 | Missing heading in a card or link embed (AP-337, owner, 2026-10-17) | Only full mode shows a section, so only full mode checks it; a card or link-mode embed of `Note#Missing` is `ok` |
| D-436 | Unresolved target at depth 4 (AP-338, owner, 2026-10-17) | An unresolved target is `broken` at any depth; `too-deep` is only for targets that resolve (FR-EMB-04 AC-5) |
| D-437 | Embed mode of an untyped note (AP-339, owner, 2026-10-17) | A note without a type (no or invalid frontmatter) embeds with the Page default, `full` (D-103, R31) |

| D-439 | Typing a mode keyword (AP-341, owner, 2026-10-17) | FR-EMB-02 AC-8's hand-typed case is an embed typed as `![[Svíčková{P}link]]` in a new paragraph (FR-EMB-01 AC-9); the source view stays read-only (D-146) |

| D-443 | No clock in L6 (AP-345, owner, 2026-10-17) | L6 checks a written body byte for byte, the frontmatter except `modified`, and `modified` as ISO 8601 with offset; the exact `modified` value stays L5. No `TEGG_NOW` |

| D-449 | User content in embed headers (AP-351, owner, 2026-10-17) | Titles, headings, attribute names and values in headers and card strips carry `data-user-content` (FR-EMB-02 AC-15) |

| D-451 | Card tests and the type store (AP-353, owner, 2026-10-17) | `Embed` reads names, preview keys and icons from the app's type store (`setChipTypes`); the L1 card tests fill it in their setup |

| D-456 | Scroll position kept (AP-358, owner, 2026-10-17) | "Scroll position kept" (FR-EMB-02 AC-10) means the content on screen stays put (WebKit's scroll anchoring adjusts `scrollTop` for growth above the view) |

| D-470 | What a new task writes (AP-400, owner, 2026-10-17) | Every new Task (quick-add, ⌘N, conversion) gets the first status with state Open (`status: To do`). Universal keys Tegg writes go after the schema attributes and template keys, `tags` then `source`, before `review`/`created`/`modified`. `newObject` takes explicit values that win over template and default (FR-TSK-10, FR-CNV-03, D-232) |
| D-471 | Missing or unknown status or state (AP-401, owner, 2026-10-17) | A task without `status`, with a value that isn't an option, or whose option has no or an unknown `state`, counts as Open and is never rewritten. | D-472 | Checkbox and `completed` (AP-402, owner, 2026-10-17) | Check → the first Done-state status and `completed` set (date-time with offset, appended after the last entry, D-230); uncheck → the first Open-state status and `completed` removed; moving between Done-state statuses keeps `completed`; Cancelled neither sets nor removes it; reading never fixes it. Done sorts by `completed`, else `modified` |
| D-473 | Editing statuses while 4E is v1.0 (AP-403, owner, 2026-10-17) | In 5A a status or priority option's colour, order and `state` can change, options can be added (with a state) and removed (values stay in files, D-471), unused labels can be renamed. Used labels are read-only (`Renaming used options comes in v1.0`, D-372). At least one Open and one Done status must remain |
| D-474 | This week and Later (AP-404, owner, 2026-10-17) | This week = tomorrow to the last day of the current week by the region's first weekday (D-329), one group per day with tasks; Later = after that, expanded; only Done is collapsed (D-379) |

Read only; changing shortcuts stays FR-SET-04 (v1.0) |
| D-609 | Lanes in the tree gates (TS-G4; owner, 2026-10-22) | G1 ran its 26 steps one after another and took 770–870 s against its 600 s budget while the Mac has 12 cores. Steps declare a `lane` in `qa.config.json`; push, nightly, milestone and release run the lanes concurrently and each lane's steps in config order; the commit gate stays sequential; `--lanes 1` (or `VERIFY_LANES=1`) restores the sequential order. Steps that share a resource (the Cargo cache, the dev-server port, a built app) share a lane. A step's output is printed as one block when it finishes, so lanes never interleave inside a step (`tools/qa/CONTRACT.md` §3.2.2). Latency ACs already run outside G1 (D-343), so CPU contention between lanes should not fail a test; it can still expose tests that don't wait for state (D-688) and the debug-build progress gap of FR-UX-07 AC-1 (open item 20). Outcome (owner, 2026-10-22): with the lanes WebKit runs on **6 workers** under `./verify` (kept by D-688; 8 saturated the machine and tipped timing-sensitive tests; supersedes the 8 of D-343); Rust AC runs use 2 threads and vitest 2 workers; lowering the cargo and node lanes' scheduling priority (`nice`) was tried and rejected because the niced lanes failed their own timing-bound tests. Full push gate on a quiet machine: 411 s. |
| D-610 | Batched, multi-threaded Rust AC runs (owner, 2026-10-22) | `cargo-junit.ts` ran one `cargo test` per AC file (35 invocations, ~46 s of overhead) with `--test-threads=1`. It now runs all AC files of a package in one invocation and splits libtest's output per binary, keeping one JUnit suite per file and the red-first assertion rule; the gates pass `--threads 4`; `crates/tegg-format/tests/ac_format.rs` runs serially (`--serial`, its FR-FMT-11 AC-2 test sets the process-wide `TZ`); `check-red` and the nightly latency ACs stay at one thread. No AC test file changes (`tools/qa/CONTRACT.md` §5.2). |
| D-611 | Step memo in G1 (owner, 2026-10-22) | Steps declare `inputs` (repository paths). The push gate skips a step whose inputs' git object ids at the gated commit, together with its command, match a recorded pass, and names the run it reuses; nightly, milestone and release always run everything and record their passes; `verify push --all` runs everything; `check-red` never uses the memo; a step without `inputs` always runs. This is content-based reuse with a declared dependency closure, not the range-based skipping the 2026-10-16 proposal rejected; the undeclared-input risk is covered by the nightly gate. Precondition: the nightly job is installed (`./verify install-nightly`, D-62). Supersedes items 4–6 of `docs/plans/verify-speedup-proposal.md` (`tools/qa/CONTRACT.md` §3.2.3). |
| D-612 | `verify merge`, the pre-merge flow (owner, 2026-10-22) | One command for what runs before a PR merges: fetch, refuse unless HEAD contains the base (`origin/main`), the push gate on HEAD (lanes and memo apply, so a merge of main reruns only the steps whose inputs changed), then `review-prep`, and a READY / NOT READY line with the tree hash the Claude hook checks before `gh pr merge` (`tools/qa/CONTRACT.md` §11). |

| D-633 | Backlinks panel chips are D-600 chips (AP-563, preview v0.55 feedback P1, owner, 2026-10-22; design board `LinkChips`) | Each chip in the Backlinks panel shows its source's D-600 states like a chip in a note: Unreviewed (amber ring, dot, description `Unreviewed`), the task box icon, the due · status suffix (Today, overdue), Done and Cancelled. The chip stays on one line and truncates the title, never the suffix; its tooltip is the full title. Amends D-368 ("type icon and title") (**the one-line part amended by D-764**) |
| D-634 | The right panel is resizable (AP-564, preview v0.55 feedback P2, owner, 2026-10-22) | The Context panel's left edge is a `separator` (`aria-orientation` vertical, label `Resize panel`, `aria-valuenow`/`min`/`max` in px). Dragging it sets the width between 240 and 480 px (default 288); when focused, ← widens and → narrows by 16 px, Home and End go to the limits; a double-click resets to 288. The width is machine-local for all vaults (`ui-state.json`, `rightPanel.width`; missing reads as 288; FR-STO-07) and restored on relaunch. The centre pane narrows by the panel's width (FR-UX-02 AC-7); the panel is drawn narrower when the centre pane would fall below 400 px and closes by itself when even 240 px doesn't fit (FR-UX-02 AC-18), without changing the stored width |
| D-635 | No Unlinked mentions in the panel (AP-565, preview v0.55 feedback P3, owner, 2026-10-22) | The Backlinks panel shows no `Unlinked mentions` section and R3 no longer lists it; FR-LNK-05 stays v1.x and comes back with its feature. Supersedes D-283 (AP-211 option b) |
| D-636 | Staged capture images and Esc (AP-566, owner, 2026-10-22; L6 run on preview v0.55) | Images and PDFs staged in the capture window (D-624) stay when the window hides (Esc, focus loss) and show again on the next open, like the text draft (FR-CAP-01 AC-8), until the capture is saved, Discard is clicked, or the window has been hidden for 10 minutes (ms6-trash-media §5); they are held in shell memory only, never in the stored draft, so they don't survive a relaunch. Discard drops them. Esc with only staged files and no text writes no draft |
| D-679 | Mindmaps drop Mermaid (AP-620, owner, 2026-10-22) | A mindmap is stored as an Obsidian callout of type `mindmap` holding one Markdown list (D-680), not as a Mermaid fence; Tegg ships no Mermaid. Other Mermaid fences stay ordinary code blocks. A `Convert to mindmap list` action for such fences is v1.0 if ever wanted. | D-680 | The mindmap marker (AP-621, owner, 2026-10-22) | A mindmap block is a callout whose type is `mindmap` (matched case-insensitively, `> [!mindmap]`, optional title, `+`/`-` fold markers as on any callout; folding writes nothing) and whose body after the title line is **exactly one list** (bulleted, numbered or checklist). Any other body (paragraphs, two lists, a list and a paragraph) is shown as an ordinary callout with the mindmap icon and the hint `A mindmap holds one list`; nothing is hidden or rewritten. `mindmap` is the 14th entry of the `Callout type` submenu (D-189) |
| D-681 | The mindmap root (AP-622, owner, 2026-10-22) | One top-level item is the root. With several top-level items the root is the callout title, or the note title when the callout has none; that root is drawn but not stored and is not editable on the map. An empty list shows the empty state `Type the central topic` with the caret in the first item |
| D-682 | What a mindmap node shows (AP-623, owner, 2026-10-22) | The item's first paragraph with its inline Markdown: bold, code, highlight, `[[links]]` as link chips, `#tags` as tag chips, date mentions. An item holding more (a second paragraph, code, a quote) shows its first line and `…`. Checklist items show their checkbox, read-only on the map. An embed `![[Task]]` in an item shows its link chip (D-600 states). Long text wraps at a fixed node width. Links in nodes are ordinary list-item links: backlinks (D-277 excerpts) and rename updates need no extractor change |
| D-683 | Tegg draws the map (AP-624, owner, 2026-10-22) | A left-to-right tree layout in the app's own code (SVG or HTML), deterministic (the same list gives the same picture, tested at L4), in Tegg's colour tokens for light and dark (FR-UX-05); each top-level branch takes a palette colour by its position. No layout, position or colour is stored. A wide map scrolls horizontally inside the block; zoom, pan, collapse and drag stay v1.0. A 500-node map lays out within one frame on the reference machine (L4 bench). Replaces the Mermaid row of the technology table and R-08's Mermaid wording **Amended by D-789, D-791:** a second layout `spider`; the layout's name is stored as callout metadata, positions and colours still never. |
| D-684 | Mindmap editing: Map and List (AP-625, owner, 2026-10-22) | The block header has a `Map` / `List` toggle (replaces the `source` toggle of D-377). **Map** shows the drawing; clicking a node switches to List with the caret in that item. **List** shows the callout body as the ordinary editable list (Tab / Shift-Tab nest, Enter adds a sibling, inline text), with the map re-rendered live below it (debounced). The block opens in Map; the chosen view lasts for the session and is never written to the file. The read view always shows Map, with links clickable **Amended by D-788:** a node click focuses the node; a double-click edits it; the list is reached with `List` or the palette. |
| D-685 | Inserting and converting mindmaps (AP-626, owner, 2026-10-22) | `/mindmap` (slash menu row, v1.0 → MS5) inserts `> [!mindmap]` with one empty item, in List, caret in the item. The block menu's `Turn into` submenu always lists `Mindmap` (after the list types) for a list and for text blocks: on a list it wraps the list in the callout unchanged; on paragraphs or headings (one block or a selection) it makes them the items of one list, as `Turn into → Bulleted list` does, and wraps that. On a mindmap, `Callout type` → another type, or `Turn into → Bulleted list`, unwraps it. Each is one undo step. FR-MMP-04 is unchanged: a type's template file can hold the block (D-92, D-270) |

| D-687 | Mindmap features left in v1.0 (AP-628, owner, 2026-10-22) | Unchanged from D-377: drag to re-parent, collapse, zoom/pan, node → object (FR-MMP-03, now FR-CNV on a list item), export as an image (FR-MMP-05, now Tegg's own drawing; FR-EXP-03). Templates beyond the verbatim file stay FR-TPL **Amended by D-785:** keyboard editing on the map and the two layouts are slice MM2; the rest stays v1.0. |
| D-688 | WebKit tests wait for state, not for time (TS-L5, owner, 2026-10-23) | Nine push gates on 2026-10-22/23 each failed a different 1–4 WebKit tests that passed alone: 6/6 with 6 workers in parallel lanes, 1/1 with 4 workers, 1/2 with the lanes run one after another (`--lanes 1`). Lane contention makes the failures more frequent, but the cause is tests acting on async state without waiting for it: a query's results, a count loaded from the core, embeds loading after Back, a save landing. **Rule (test-strategy §8.4):** a UI test waits for the state it acts on or reads (`settled` for a list showing the typed query, `eventually` for a value from the core, `must` for an element); a fixed sleep is only allowed before asserting that nothing happened. WebKit stays on 6 workers under `./verify` (D-609); a 4-worker trial slowed the WebKit step by ~170 s without fewer failures. A test that still flakes is quarantined as §8.4 says: the Playwright tag `@flaky` keeps it out of the gates' `app-webkit` run and the nightly step `app-webkit-flaky` runs it (first: `fr_emb_02_ac9_card_and_link_mode_open_their_target`, issue #59) |
| D-689 | A conversion that finishes late (FR-CNV-03, FR-CNV-06; owner, 2026-10-23; issue #61) | The object file is created before the source's blocks are replaced, so the replacement can land after the user moved on. When it lands, it selects the new embed and scrolls to it only if the editor's selection is unchanged since Convert; otherwise the user's selection stays where it is (mapped past the replaced blocks) and the note doesn't scroll. Keeping the content on screen while embeds load (D-456) stops as soon as anything else scrolls the note, so it can't undo a caret move; an empty paragraph the user made, which a reload drops, keeps the caret near where it was (#64). If the source changed under the converted blocks meanwhile (`convertUnit` refuses), the new file is discarded while it is still as created and the `convert.failed` alert says nothing was changed; a file changed since is moved to the trash with the undo alert instead (as FR-CNV-06 AC-3). The `/task` on an empty paragraph (no unit) is not covered yet: issue #62 |
| D-690 | Tag page grouping (AP-629, owner, 2026-10-22; design board `TagPage`) | The tag page is one flat list of every object with the tag, whatever its type (FR-TAG-06). The toolbar's grouping button (`No grouping`) opens a menu `No grouping` / `Type`; `Type` groups the rows by type (D-122 order of the type names), each group with its count. `Type: all` filters by type, it doesn't group. Default `No grouping` |
| D-691 | No tag description in the MVP (AP-630, owner, 2026-10-22) | The tag page and the All tags list show no description; amends FR-TAG-01, FR-TAG-03, FR-TAG-06 and D-370 for the MVP. A `description:` already in a Tag file is preserved, never shown or rewritten. AP-631 (a description column on All tags) is withdrawn |

| D-694 | How a tag finds its Tag file (AP-633, owner, 2026-10-22) | A Tag file is an object with `type: tag` in `Tags/`. It belongs to the tag whose key (`tag_key`: NFC, lower case) equals the key of its `title`; the file name is only the sanitized title (D-51, FR-FMT-08), so `#a/b` lives in `Tags/a b.md`. A Tag file without `title` uses its file stem. Two files for one key: the oldest `created` wins (then path order), the others are listed on the Duplicate titles page. A tag without a file is virtual (FR-TAG-03). The index keeps the mapping; deleting and rebuilding it gives the same result (AR-01) |
| D-695 | What a new Tag file holds (AP-634, owner, 2026-10-22) | `id, type: tag, title: <spelling>, color, created, modified` in the reserved-key order (`color` only when one is chosen, D-707); no `description`, no body. Written atomically (FR-SYN-02) in `Tags/`, the name sanitized and suffixed ` (2)` if taken (FR-LNK-14) |

A new tag without objects is listed with count 0 and offered by tag autocomplete (FR-EDT-15) and quick-add (D-380). Picking a colour for a virtual tag also creates its file (D-707) |

| D-749 | MS4 milestone gate fixes (owner, 2026-10-23) | (1) Tree gates link the main checkout's `app/e2e/.appium` (the Appium home with the Mac2 driver, gitignored) into the worktree, like `node_modules`; without it every real-app test fails to start a session. (2) Property tests that run 10 seeds at `QA_PROPERTY_SCALE` ≥ 100 get a time limit that grows with the scale instead of Vitest's 5 s. (3) The Rust rename property test keeps one Node `transform-cli` process for the whole run instead of one per case, and a job's report no longer waits for its 200 ms progress heartbeat to end (every job finished up to 200 ms late; regression test `bug_ms4gate_fr_ux_07_…`). (4) The tasks benchmark matches `type:` case-insensitively, as the app does (the reference vault writes `type: Task`). (5) The quarantined WebKit tests (`app-webkit-flaky`, D-688) are reported, not a gate failure, until fixed; T1 tests still may not stay quarantined past the milestone (test-strategy §8.4). `fr_emb_02_ac9_card_and_link_mode_open_their_target` leaves the quarantine (issue #59): it clicked while Back still restored the scroll, so the mouse-up landed on another embed; the two FR-EMB-02 AC-9 tests wait for the scroll to settle (D-688). (6) The `tag-rows:<n>` (AP-674) and `backlinks-painted:<path>` (ms4-links §5) marks the benchmarks read are built |

ACs of 4E (v1.0, D-372) lose their `[MS4]` markers: links.md FR-STO-04 AC-1, AC-2, FR-TYP-04 AC-1 (struck: it returns with 4E's ACs), types.md FR-TYP-05 AC-4, data-safety FR-STO-01 AC-7. backlinks.md FR-LNK-01 AC-5, AC-6 (excerpts) are retired by D-368 (chips; AC-19) |

The capture tag-colour test adds its tag in `Add tag…`; the tag page test picks the `Type` menu item (the menu and the column share the name); the merge test presses the Schůzka group's `Merge…`; the broken-chip lists drop `Smazaná` (a trashed chip, D-615, D-628); the drop test drops on the note's body; the text tolerance of the chip colour check is 24. The test build gets the hooks `TEGG_TEST_DROP` (in the 6A contract, never built) and `TEGG_TEST_FOCUS_TITLE` (the title has the focus once the note is ready, for the tests that rename without Appium). A capture tag chip reads `#<tag>` as one text |
| D-752 | Real-app text queries never compare a number (owner, 2026-10-24: investigate the missing interrupted-rename banner as a bug) | The banner was shown; the crash test's query `label CONTAINS … OR value CONTAINS …` made XCTest's agent inside Tegg throw on a numeric `value` (a progress bar while the index catches up, a checkbox) and the app died, so 7 of 20 FR-LNK-03 AC-17 cycles reported "no interrupted banner". `value` is compared only for static text, text fields and text views (helper `textContains`, and the same rule in ms3_shell, ms4_rename and nightly_rename_crash); regression test `bug_ms4gate_fr_lnk_03_ac17_…`. Not an app bug |
| D-753 | The tag crash test kills during `writing` (owner, 2026-10-24: "then the tag crash test") | FR-TAG-04 AC-10 kills "at a random time during `writing`"; the test killed 0.3–3.3 s after ⏎, sometimes before the zip and the first write, when there is nothing to restore and no zip is listed. It now waits for the first rewritten file, then kills within 3 s; 20 of 20 cycles pass |
| D-754 | Vault writes keep `F_FULLFSYNC` (AP-707, owner, 2026-10-24) | Measured on the reference Mac: a full flush costs 7.6 ms per linking file (note write and journal append), about 4 s of the 53.5 s 500-link rename (FR-UX-07 AC-11); a build with plain `fsync` took 59.5 s, no faster. Plain `fsync` could leave a torn note after a power loss (FR-SYN-02), `F_BARRIERFSYNC` would save about 3 s. So every atomic write keeps `F_FULLFSYNC` for the file and its folder. The crash test's Finish gets 3 minutes (FR-LNK-03 AC-17). The real cost (one JS text-engine round trip per file, lock contention with the indexer) is a separate performance follow-up (issue #67) |
| D-755 | Finish and Undo after a kill between the note's rename and its journal line (bug, MS4 milestone gate; owner asked to check the no-progress cycle, 2026-10-24) | The engine renames the note, then logs it; a kill in between left the note renamed and the journal saying "pending". Finish looked for the note at its old path, found nothing, rewrote no link and removed the journal (the real-app crash test: the note renamed, 500 links to the old name, nothing left to offer); Undo couldn't move it back. Now a pending note that is gone from its old path and is at its target path with the same `id` counts as renamed: Finish rewrites the links, Undo renames it back with its old bytes. Regression tests `bug_ms4gate_fr_lnk_03_ac17_…` (Rust) |

| D-765 | The title line and the first item (AP-726, owner, 2026-10-25; slice MM, `docs/acceptance/mindmap.md`) | (a) For a callout of type `mindmap`, Tegg reads the lines after the title line as the body on their own (Obsidian's reading): Rozpoznání (5) and (6) are mindmaps. | D-766 | How much an edit rewrites (AP-727, owner, 2026-10-25; slice MM, `docs/acceptance/mindmap.md`) | Accept the block-level rewrite (as for lists): bytes outside the callout never change, the rewritten callout reads back to the same tree with only the edited item changed, and once canonical, later edits change only the edited lines (FR-MMP-02 AC-18). |
| D-767 | Wrap and unwrap bytes, and the title on unwrap (AP-728, owner, 2026-10-25; slice MM, `docs/acceptance/mindmap.md`) | Wrap = prefix every line of the list's source with `> ` (`>` for an empty line) and add the title line above; the list's own bytes are kept. Unwrap = remove the prefixes and the title line; a non-empty title becomes a paragraph above the list, so no text is lost. `Turn into › Bulleted list / Numbered list / Checklist` unwraps and gives the list that kind (no change when it already is; otherwise re-rendered in that kind, as `Turn into` on a list does). An untitled wrap followed by an unwrap returns the original bytes. |
| D-768 | Where `Turn into › Mindmap` is offered (AP-729, owner, 2026-10-25; slice MM, `docs/acceptance/mindmap.md`) | Listed after `Checklist` for one list, one paragraph or heading, and a selection of only paragraphs and headings; on a mindmap it is shown checked and does nothing; not listed for quotes, callouts (they use `Callout type`), code, tables, embeds, dividers and source blocks; `aria-disabled` for a selection that mixes a list with other blocks or holds two lists. |
| D-769 | Nested and embedded mindmaps; `/mindmap` inside containers (AP-730, owner, 2026-10-25; slice MM, `docs/acceptance/mindmap.md`) | Drawn only as a top-level block of a note's body, also inside an embedded note shown in full or as a section; nested in a quote, callout or list item it is an ordinary callout with the mindmap icon and no hint. `/mindmap` inside a list item, quote, callout or mindmap inserts the new mindmap after the containing top-level block, caret in its item (as pasted blocks go after a callout). |
| D-770 | Keyboard and pointer on the map (AP-731, owner, 2026-10-25; slice MM, `docs/acceptance/mindmap.md`) | The map is a `tree` `Map of <title>`, one Tab stop with roving focus: ↑/↓ outline order, → first child, ← parent, Home/End. ⏎ or Space = node click (editor: List with the caret; read view: nothing); ⌘↩ opens the node's first link in both views. Link chips are pointer targets that open their link, not Tab stops; a click elsewhere on a node is the node click. The checkbox is drawn (`aria-hidden`), the node's description says `Checked` / `Not checked`, and a click on it is a node click, never a toggle. `Map` and `List` are Tab-reachable buttons bound to new palette commands `Show mindmap as map` / `Show mindmap as list` (no shortcut). **Amended by D-787, D-788, D-790:** ⏎ and Space edit instead of switching to List; a click focuses; ← / → follow the screen in the spider layout. |
| D-771 | The title, and a click on a synthesized root (AP-732, owner, 2026-10-25; slice MM, `docs/acceptance/mindmap.md`) | The title is the header's text: editable in place in List (single line, like a callout title, FR-EDT-02 AC-19), read-only in Map; only the first line is written, `> [!mindmap] <title>`, and an emptied title writes `> [!mindmap]` with no trailing space; the fold marker and the type's spelling are kept; an empty title shows `Mindmap` in `muted`. Clicking a synthesized root switches to List with the caret at the end of the title when the root is the callout title, or at the start of the first item when it is the note title (edited in the note's Title field). |
| D-772 | List keys that would break "one list" (AP-733, owner, 2026-10-25; slice MM, `docs/acceptance/mindmap.md`) | Inside a mindmap: ⏎ on an empty top-level item removes it and puts the caret in a new empty paragraph after the block; ⇧Tab on a top-level item does nothing; ⌫ at the start of the only, empty item turns the block into an empty paragraph (one undo step). Blocks nested in an item (a second paragraph, `/code`) stay allowed and show `…` on the map. |
| D-773 | Paste into a mindmap (AP-734, owner, 2026-10-25; slice MM, `docs/acceptance/mindmap.md`) | In List: a clipboard that parses to only lists, paragraphs and headings becomes items after the caret's item at its level (nested lists keep their nesting, paragraphs and headings become items); a single line is inserted at the caret; a clipboard with any other block goes after the mindmap block (the current rule). In Map, paste does nothing. |
| D-774 | Node content details (AP-735, owner, 2026-10-25; slice MM, `docs/acceptance/mindmap.md`) | (1) The whole first paragraph is drawn, wrapped; a hard break is a line break; ` …` is appended when the item holds other blocks besides its child lists, with the description `More in the list`. (2) An item without text is an empty node (48 px). (3) Image, PDF and other attachment embeds show a file chip (`image` / `file` icon and the file name), never the picture. (4) Numbered lists draw no numbers (order is top to bottom). (5) An item's children are the items of every list directly inside it, in file order. |
| D-775 | Geometry, branch palette, connectors, block height (AP-736, owner, 2026-10-25; slice MM, `docs/acceptance/mindmap.md`) | R92's numbers, taken from the Main board (32 px one-line nodes, 60 px column gap, 8 px row gap, elbow connectors, width up to 200 px, wrapping at 180 px of text); branch palette `blue`, `orange`, `purple`, `teal` (the board's four), `green`, `yellow`, `red`, then again; nodes below the first level neutral; connectors 1.5 px in `muted`; no maximum block height (the page scrolls; the map scrolls only horizontally). |
| D-776 | View state, caret on a node click, live re-render (AP-737, owner, 2026-10-25; slice MM, `docs/acceptance/mindmap.md`) | Session = while the app runs: kept in memory per note `id` (path for files without one) and the block's ordinal among the note's mindmaps, never in `ui-state.json`; it survives navigation and external reloads and resets on relaunch and vault switch; a mindmap made by `/mindmap` or `Turn into` starts in List. A node click puts the caret at the end of the item's first paragraph. The map under the list re-lays out 150 ms after the last change, so it reflects the list within 500 ms. **Amended by D-786, D-788:** a map edit re-lays out at once; a node click no longer puts the caret in the list. |
| D-777 | Test layers of the layout and the frame budget (AP-738, owner, 2026-10-25; slice MM, `docs/acceptance/mindmap.md`) | The layout is a pure function tested at L1 against R92's golden values with the Measurer; L5 checks the rendered boxes against it; the 500-node budget is the layout's p95 over 20 runs ≤ 16.7 ms (one 60 Hz frame, no D-59 factor, D-66), reported at L5 on every push and at L6 on the reference machine, report-only until MS8 (D-152). |
| D-778 | Block → object inside a mindmap (AP-739, owner, 2026-10-25; slice MM, `docs/acceptance/mindmap.md`) | In a mindmap's list, `Turn into object` (toolbar, slash, ⇧⌘T) is `aria-disabled` with `Coming in v1.0`; the block menu's `Turn into object` on the whole mindmap block works as for any callout. |

| D-787 | Keys on the map (AP-743, owner, 2026-10-26; slice MM2; amends D-770) | With a node focused: ⏎ adds an empty sibling after it and edits it (on the root: the first child; on a synthesized root: a new top-level item at the end); Tab adds an empty child at the end of the node's children and edits it; Space or F2 edits the node's text; ⌫ or Delete removes a node without children (focus to the previous sibling, else the parent) and does nothing on a node with children; ⌥↑ / ⌥↓ move the node among its siblings; ⌥→ nests it under the previous sibling, ⌥← un-nests it after its parent; ⌘↩ opens the first link (D-770). An item with more than its first paragraph (D-774) edits its first paragraph only. No autocomplete, floating toolbar or date mention on the map (v1.0). Pointer: a single click focuses the node (it no longer switches to List), a double-click edits its text, a click on a chip opens its link (D-770). Inserting a first-level branch in the middle flips the side of every later branch: accepted, because the side is a function of the outline position, never of history (R-08). |

A first-level branch appended at the end lands on the lighter side by D-789's alternation. |

| D-900 | Graph edges (AP-686, owner, 2026-10-28) | An edge joins two objects when one refers to the other by a **link** in the body (`[[…]]`; `#Heading` and `#^block` links count as links to the object), a **relation** property value, an **embed** (`![[…]]`; an attachment embed counts as an embed of its Image or PDF object, as backlinks do, FR-ATT-07), a **tag** (`tags:` or inline `#tag`, to the Tag node) or a **date mention** of an existing Daily note. Not edges: unresolved links and dates without a Daily note (no ghost nodes), links to itself, anything in `_templates/` (FR-TPL-07) or `.trash/` (FR-GRP-01) |
| D-901 | Graph connection toggles (AP-687, owner, 2026-10-28) | Four toggles with the same labels in the full view and the panel: `Links`, `Relations`, `Embeds`, `Tags`. A link counts in both directions (outgoing and backlink); there are no separate outgoing / backlink toggles. FR-GRP-02 is reworded |
| D-902 | One graph edge per pair, no arrows (AP-688, owner, 2026-10-28) | Two objects joined in several ways get one line, drawn in the style of the strongest kind (embed > relation > link > tag); no arrowheads. Direction and every kind are named in words in the preview header (`Linked from <title> by relation <Name>`), VoiceOver and the List view |
| D-903 | How the graph's depth is walked (AP-689, owner, 2026-10-28) | Breadth-first from the centre, following edges in both directions; a node's depth is its shortest distance. **Tag nodes and Daily notes are leaves**: shown when reached, not walked through |
| D-904 | Graph filters and the walk (AP-690, owner, 2026-10-28) | The walk follows only visible types and connection kinds, so every drawn node is connected to the centre through drawn nodes. The centre is always drawn, whatever its type. The depth control (`Depth 1 · 2 hidden`) and the type counts report how many objects the filter removed at this depth |
| D-905 | Graph default depth (AP-691, owner, 2026-10-28) | Both views start at depth 1 (FR-GRP-01); depth is remembered per view with the filters (D-906). The full view opened from the panel takes the panel's depth; opened from the palette or ⌘⇧G it uses its own remembered depth |
| D-906 | Where graph state is saved (AP-692, owner, 2026-10-28) | Per-view state (depth, type and connection filters, node size, Graph or List, current preset) is machine-local in `ui-state.json` under `graph.panel` and `graph.full`, per vault, not per object (FR-STO-07). Presets are portable, in the vault as `.tegg/graph.yml` (FR-STO-06), written atomically and only when a preset is saved, updated, renamed or deleted (FR-SYN-02, -06) |
| D-907 | Graph preset rules (AP-693, owner, 2026-10-28; board `GraphDetails` D) | One preset list for both views; a preset holds types and connection kinds, not depth. `Default` (all types, all connections) is built in, not stored, not renamable. A preset stores the **hidden** types, so a type created later shows in every preset. Names are unique (case-insensitive, NFC) and not empty. An unknown type key in `graph.yml` is ignored and kept on save; an unreadable `graph.yml` gives only `Default` and is never rewritten without a user change |
| D-908 | Graph node size (AP-694, owner, 2026-10-28) | `Size nodes by links` (FR-GRP-07) is off by default in both views. When on, size follows the object's number of connections in the whole vault, in three steps, so it doesn't change with the depth |
| D-909 | Tag nodes (AP-695, owner, 2026-10-28; board `GraphDetails` A) | A tag node is a pill `#name`: tint fill, border and text from the tag's colour token (D-707), `None` as the neutral chip; its edges are thin and dotted. Virtual tags (no Tag file, D-694) are shown too. A click previews the Tag object, a double-click opens its tag page. This meets the graph part of FR-TAG-05 and D-707 (FR-GRP-08) |
| D-910 | The graph legend (AP-696, owner, 2026-10-28) | The filter list is the legend (icon, colour, name, count): the full view's filter column, and in the panel the type list of the depth and filters popover. No separate legend strip. FR-GRP-05 is reworded |
| D-911 | Neighbourhood query in the core (AP-697, owner, 2026-10-28) | A platform-neutral query in `tegg-index` (AR-03): `graph_neighbourhood(centre, depth, visible types, visible kinds, cap)` returns nodes (path, title, type, depth, vault-wide degree, tag colour), edges (kinds, direction), the hidden count, the total and a truncated flag. A new request cancels the running one. Rebuilding the index gives the same result (AR-01). Recorded in `docs/architecture.md` with G1 |
| D-912 | Graph size caps and speed (AP-698, owner, 2026-10-28; board `GraphStates`) | The panel draws at most 60 nodes and above that offers a lower depth or the full view; the full view draws at most 150 (most connected first; the centre and depth 1 always kept) with a banner offering `Filter types…` and `Show all`. `Show all` draws up to 1,000 nodes, once, not remembered; above 1,000 only a lower depth or filters are offered. Labels hide above 60 visible nodes and show on zoom or hover. New **NFR-PERF-11**: the local graph at depth 2 is drawn in < 300 ms p95 on the reference vault (query and layout); the loading state appears only after 300 ms. `Show all` is outside the target |
| D-913 | Graph layout and library (AP-699, owner, 2026-10-28) | Force-directed layout with **d3-force** (MIT, bundled, no network, NFR-SEC-01), drawn as SVG with one focusable element per node (D-918). The centre is pinned in the middle; the layout is seeded, so the same graph at the same size gives the same picture; it runs to rest before the first paint, with no settling animation under Reduce motion (FR-UX-08). Positions are not stored. On a live update surviving nodes keep their place and new ones start next to a neighbour. ADR 0004 records it when G1 starts |
| D-914 | Full-view graph route (AP-700, owner, 2026-10-28) | Route `graph/<path>` (NFC, as `note/<path>`), tab title `Graph · <title>`, built in G1 (app-shell R1). It opens in a new tab from the panel, the palette and ⌘⇧G. Centring on another node (`Center graph here`, Recenter, `C`) changes the route's centre and pushes a history entry, so Back returns to the previous centre. After a rename (FR-LNK-05) the route follows the new path |
| D-915 | Graph live updates and deleted objects (AP-701, owner, 2026-10-28) | The graph redraws, debounced, when the index reports a change to any drawn object, within NFR-PERF-08. A selected node that disappears loses the selection and its preview closes. If the centre is deleted, the full view shows `This object was deleted` with `Back`; the panel shows nothing for it (board `GraphStates`, new state) |
| D-916 | Graph keyboard, VoiceOver and List view (AP-702, owner, 2026-10-28; board `GraphDetails` C) | Tab lands on the centre node; arrows move to the nearest node in that direction; Return previews (per FR-LNK-12); ⌘Return opens, ⌘⇧Return in a new tab; `C` centres; `+` `−` `0` zoom in, out, fit; Esc closes the preview, then leaves the graph. Single keys work only while focus is in the graph. VoiceOver reads title, type, connection count, how it is linked to the centre and position (`2 of 9`). A `Graph` / `List` switch exists in **both** views, remembered per view (D-906); the List view is the same neighbourhood as an indented tree (FR-UX-03, FR-UX-08) |
| D-917 | Preview in the graph (AP-703, owner, 2026-10-28) | The graph uses the standard content-first preview (FR-LNK-10) unchanged: in the full view's right column, in the panel as the popover beside the panel (board `GraphPanel` C). The graph adds one line under the type: `<Type> · <n> connections`. Hover mode uses the same preview, not a compact card; the ring fill during the hover delay and the 300 ms grace when leaving stay as drawn (FR-GRP-06) |
| D-918 | Graph shortcut and palette commands (AP-704, owner, 2026-10-28) | ⌘⇧G `Open graph` on any object route (the full view of the current object; nothing in Tegg uses ⌘⇧G). Palette: `Show local graph` (the panel on Graph), `Open graph`, `Graph: Depth 1` / `2` / `3`, `Graph: Recenter`, `Graph: Apply preset…`, `Graph: Show as list` / `Show as graph`; the `Graph:` commands are listed only while a graph is open (FR-NAV-02) |

| D-924 | Popover kind, focus and closing (AP-806) (owner, 2026-10-28; slice P, `docs/acceptance/previews.md`) | The preview is always a non-modal `dialog` named `Preview: <title>`. A click outside closes it and still does what it does. A `Close preview` (×) button. One popup at a time (a preview, the Create offer or a menu) |

| D-1080 | The `graph.yml` format (AP-920) (owner, 2026-10-30; slice G3, `docs/acceptance/graph.md`) | R118: a header comment, `version: 1` (AR-07, as type files; missing = 1), `presets:` a list of `{ name, hidden-types, hidden-kinds }` with kebab-case keys as other vault YAML; a preset is identified by its name (no id), so the file stays readable by hand. |
| D-1081 | How Tegg writes the file (AP-921) (owner, 2026-10-30; slice G3, `docs/acceptance/graph.md`) | Minimal line patches (R118 write rules): only the operated item's lines change; changed lists become one-line flow lists (a block list is replaced, a trailing comment on its line kept); types in D-977 order then unknown keys in their old order; kinds `link, relation, embed, tag`; line endings and the final newline as the file had them; the `vault.yml` quoting rule (D-230). |
| D-1082 | Unreadable files and bad entries (AP-922) (owner, 2026-10-30; slice G3, `docs/acceptance/graph.md`) | While the file is unreadable, every editing action is disabled with `Fix .tegg/graph.yml to save presets.`, so Tegg never replaces it; in a readable file, bad entries (no or blank or non-string name, `Default`, a case or NFC duplicate, a non-list `hidden-*`) are skipped and kept byte for byte; the first of duplicates wins. |
| D-1083 | A newer `version` (AP-923) (owner, 2026-10-30; slice G3, `docs/acceptance/graph.md`) | Same as type files: presets are listed and can be applied; save, update, rename, delete are disabled with `Made by a newer version of Tegg. | D-1085 | Sync conflict copies of `graph.yml` (AP-925) (owner, 2026-10-30; slice G3, `docs/acceptance/graph.md`) | Read only `.tegg/graph.yml`; never read, write, rename or delete a copy, and say nothing in G3 (the owner merges by hand, as for type files). |

| D-1120 | Unreadable line of an unclosed flow collection (owner, 2026-10-30; G3 build) | When the YAML error falls inside a `[` or `{` still open, `Unreadable.line` is the line where the outermost one opened (`invalid-yaml.yml` → line 5); otherwise the parser's 1-based line |

| D-1122 | Files that can't be patched by lines (owner, 2026-10-30; G3 build) | A readable file whose presets can't be located line by line (a non-empty flow `presets: [...]`, a `- {name: x}` item, a quoted key the scanner can't find, items that don't line up with the parsed YAML) lists its presets but every write is refused with `Unreadable`, so the file is never rewritten |
| D-1123 | Name check order (owner, 2026-10-30; G3 build) | `NameControl` on the raw input (any Cc character, so a leading or trailing tab is refused), then trimming of Unicode White_Space (not U+FEFF), NFC, `NameEmpty`, `NameTooLong` (> 100 scalar values), `NameTaken`; matching compares `nfc(trim(s))` lower-cased |
| D-1124 | Indentation of new items and the version line (owner, 2026-10-30; G3 build) | A saved item copies the last existing item's dash and key indentation (2 / 4 spaces when there is none); a missing `version: 1` goes after the leading run of comment lines and is added only by an operation that changes the file |

| D-1126 | G3 red-test corrections (owner, 2026-10-30; G3 build) | `graph-helpers.ts` `menuRadio` matches the name up to a space or the end (`(?=\s|$)`, as JavaScript's `\b` fails after `ů`, `í`); FR-UX-03 AC-27's last step uses a fresh page; FR-UX-03 AC-23 and AC-25 wait for the graph to be ready before reading the route marks. Applied with the `AC-Test-Change` trailer |
| D-1127 | When `route-ready:graph/<path>` is marked (owner, 2026-10-30; G3 build) | In the same commit that first shows the result (a layout effect), not after the next frame; `graph-ready` keeps its frame wait. Amends contract §3 |
| D-1128 | AR-03 list in the presets test (owner, 2026-10-30; G3 push gate) | `fr_sto_06_ac1_presets_are_a_vault_file_only` builds its forbidden strings from parts (`concat!("tau", "ri::")`), so the repository's AR-03 scan of test files (`ar_03_ac4_…`) doesn't flag the test itself; applied with the `AC-Test-Change` trailer |
| D-1129 | Comments, safe writes and unpatchable files in `graph.yml` (owner, 2026-10-30; G3 QA review) | Comment and blank lines never end a preset or a value range, at any column: a preset runs from its `- ` line to its last content line (comments inside it go with it on delete, comments after it stay); a block value runs over its deeper lines and compact `- ` lines, and comments between them are replaced on update. Before every write the new text is parsed back and must give exactly the expected presets and other keys, else the write is refused with `Unreadable`. A leading BOM stays first; `version: 1` goes after leading comments, `%` directives and the first `---`. A file with several YAML documents or a flow item (`- {…}`) lists its presets but is never written; an item that defines an anchor used elsewhere can't be deleted or updated (D-1122) |

| D-1330 | One `./verify` gate at a time (owner, 2026-10-30) | Parallel gates of several sessions loaded the machine (load average up to 44) and made timing-sensitive WebKit tests fail falsely. The heavy commands (`push`, `nightly`, `milestone`, `release`, `merge`, `check-red`, and the pre-push hook when it runs the push gate) take a machine-wide lock and wait for it; `verify commit` doesn't. The lock is a folder outside every checkout, released on exit, and taken over when its owner process is gone. Contract `tools/qa/CONTRACT.md` §3.5 |

Inside groups 1 and 2: exact words before typo or prefix matches (D-101, D-1342), then bm25, then path. Quoted phrases must still match somewhere and `-word` still excludes (D-253); D-1205 (filters only) is unchanged. Amends D-253; restores D-107 |

| D-1355 | L6 clicks at the element's centre (owner, 2026-11-01: "Yes, record and merge"; L6 run on main + `claude/l6-harness`) | With Xcode 27.2 on macOS 26.6, XCTest's element click asserts in SkyLight and kills WebDriverAgent for elements inside Tegg's web content, and every later session fails. The L6 harness (`app/e2e/helpers/l6-guard.ts`, loaded with `node --import` by every L6 step) turns every element click, double-click and element-based `macos:` gesture into a mouse event at the element's centre (D-751 did this only where XCTest's click does nothing). Trade-off: a centre click doesn't check hittability, so a covered element fails at the next assertion instead of at the click. The harness also restarts a dead WebDriverAgent, quits a leftover test build, and fails at once on a locked screen. No AC test changes. |

It is versioned like `graph.yml` (D-906). The collapsed state stays machine-local (AP-1240). |
| D-1371 | File format and patch rules (AP-1228) (owner, 2026-11-07: "Accept all"; slice PIN, `docs/acceptance/pinned.md`) | R131: `version: 1` and `pins:` with one item per pin, patched line by line like `graph.yml` (D-1081: minimal patch, quoting, CRLF, final newline, comments and unknown keys kept, never deleted, last unpin leaves `pins: []`, a conflict refuses and reloads with no automatic retry). |
| D-1372 | What a pin refers to (AP-1229) (owner, 2026-11-07: "Accept all"; slice PIN, `docs/acceptance/pinned.md`) | An object is stored as `id` + `path` (`path` only when the file has no `id`), resolved by `id` first and `path` second. A path whose file now has another `id` is never adopted. Pinning never writes the note. A tag is stored as its key, a type page as its type key. |
| D-1373 | Renames, moves and tag renames (AP-1230) (owner, 2026-11-07: "Accept all"; slice PIN, `docs/acceptance/pinned.md`) | Tegg's rename job (4C), its 4E moves and type changes patch the pin's `path:` line, and add `id:` when the rename wrote one. The tag-rename job patches `tag:` lines. All of these happen inside the job, with the job's base check, backup and Undo. External renames are followed by `id` with no write: the stale path is a hint and stays until Tegg patches that item. |
| D-1374 | Trashed, deleted and missing targets (AP-1231) (owner, 2026-11-07: "Accept all"; slice PIN, `docs/acceptance/pinned.md`) | The row stays, with the state `In the trash` (it opens `trash`) or `Not found` (an alert with `Unpin`). It comes back by itself on restore or when the file reappears. A tag with no objects reads `No objects` and opens D-720's page. A pin is **never removed automatically**, not even by Tegg's own "delete to the macOS Trash": a sync client may remove and re-add files, and an automatic removal would be a silent write. |

A Tag file pins as its tag. Also any tag (virtual ones too) and any type page (also an undefined type with objects). Not pinnable: routes (Inbox, Today, Tasks, Search, All tags, graph views, Settings), objects in `.trash/`, templates, and unsaved objects (`new/<type>`, a virtual daily note), where `Pin to sidebar` is aria-disabled `Available once the object is saved.` |

It is never written to the vault. |

The board's `Convert to…` is today's `Change type…`. |

A newer version is listed read-only. Sync conflict copies are ignored. Invalid items are skipped and kept. |

An unpin never removes other items |
